  <snip>
  >Your assertions that because with cut outs you can explain how
  >such photos may have been produced, does not of course prove
  >that this was the way it happened - no more than counterfeit
  >bank notes prove all notes are faked.
  Hello Dave, you should read this in HAL9000's voice...
  Indeed. But when we have convincing evidence that that was the
  way he hoaxed before, then the assertion gains more weight. See
  below.
  >Similarly statements that Broeke is a 'noted hoaxer' seem to be
  >based on little, other than suspicions of faking or attempts to
  >show how he may have hoaxed.
  Did you see his alien which was in fact a cutout from Reader's
  Digest, that is, he used a common available photo of which the
  source was located? This is no suspicion of hoax, I don't think
  any reasonable person would believe his excuse that he somehow
  psychically materialized the exact photo published on a magazine
  in front of his camera.
  http://www.skepsis.nl/mudman.html
  >My position is this. Apart from laboratory experiments in which
  >Broeke is monitored by witnesses and cameras to document these
  >photos paranormally appearing on camera screens, I don't see how
  >anybody can prove or disprove this case.
  In several of these more recent photos one can see a line
  connecting the "UFO" to the border of the image. And the


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/nov/m13-006.shtml[12/12/2011 09:39:00]
Re: BLT Research Medium's UFO Photos

  anomalies always appears at the extremities of the photo frame.
  That's because it's Broeke simply holding things in front of his
  camera. He doesn't even throw them in the air.
  This cannot be definitely proven unless we record him actually
  doing it, true, but again, as I originally wrote, the fact the
  images reflect back the camera flash is indeed already proof
  they were very near the camera (as the flash range is limited),
  ergo, they are really small objects. Furthermore, from the way
  some of the objects reflects the flash, one can also see clear
  evidence they are flat objects. So one has to assume these were
  either real miniature, bidimensional alien spaceships flying in
  front of the camera, or that Broeke was holding cutouts in front
  of the camera,
  And we have clear cases where Broeke used common available
  photos as cutouts and claimed them to be real alien images. The
  conclusion is inevitable.
  >Of course the similarities to the Meier photos are to be noted,
  >which probably works in favour rather than against Broeke, in
  >the sense that who would make cut outs of discredited Meier when
  >you could sculpt some impressive paper discs of your own?
  I don't know. I know that Broeke used a cutout from Reader's
  Digest, which he should have imagined someone would eventually
  find out. But he took not one but several photos of it and
  claimed them to be a real alien materialized in his house.
  Broeke is a hoaxer. Talbott seems to genuinely believe him, even
  though she knows about the Reader's Digest and other objections
  aired about his photos.

Re: Conclusion Of NASA Kecksburg Lawsuit




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Nov > Nov 13

                                        Re: Conclusion Of NASA Kecksburg Lawsuit
  From: Steven Kaeser <steve.nul>
  Date: Thu, 12 Nov 2009 13:52:11 -0500
  Archived: Fri, 13 Nov 2009 19:15:11 -0500
  Subject: Re: Conclusion Of NASA Kecksburg Lawsuit

  >From: Bruce Maccabee <brumac.nul>
  >To: post.nul
  >Date: Wed, 11 Nov 2009 13:48:32 -0500
  >Subject: Re: Conclusion Of NASA Kecksburg Lawsuit
  ><snip>
  >Thanks for the report of "major frustration."
  >'Lost records' remind me of the lost communication records from
  >Roswell Army Air Force Base for a time period that include July
  >1947.
  Hi Bruce,
  I share the frustration, but there are two points to note
  As I recall, when it was learned that Roswell communications
  records had been destroyed, along with the records authorizing
  that action, a check of communications records at other base
  records in general from that time period, this seemed to be
  widespread. A number of gaps in the communications records were
  found, and this may have been systemic at the time, rather than
  a cover-up. I'm not sure how we show that the Roswell
  disappearance can be shown to be different.
  NASA, on the other hand, couldn't keep track of the original
  moon landing data and recently acknowledged that they apparently
  erased most of it to re-use the tapes and save money. Given how
  long ago archived data was moved to Optical Disk storage, this
  shows how long ago this tragic decision was made. And, the fact
  that it took over a decade to figure out what happened to the
  data, was also telling. Not only was NASA cheap, but they also
  couldn't keep good records about what data they had and what
  archive it was stored in - as they moved it around to less
  expensive warehouses.
  I was in Congressman John Murtha's Office in the mid-90s and
  talked with the legislative aide that was assigned all
  Kecksburg/UFO inquiries. The Congressman's LA said that he
  learned little from the military, but indicated that the
  accepted cause was the Soviet Satellite theory. That would
  confirm that there was an Event that occurred. But, this was all
  based on third-hand hearsay and showed that the Congressman -
  who doesn't have clearance to get any military operational data
  - didn't really know anything other than what everyone has found
  outside the cone of secrecy.
  CFI is to be commended for keeping a focus on this, and we
  appear to be dealing with two Agencies that are either covering
  their rear-ends, or are inept at good record keeping. The
  Military could have the Cold War to blame for the extreme
  classification of this incident, or it could well be some alien
  technology that fell from the sky. Both would likely result in a
  compartmentalized classification group. But I tend to support
  the incompetence or poor management of data by that Agency, as
  to why they can't find, or know what records they even have.
  There's a lot of fog to make your way through in this case, and


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/nov/m13-008.shtml[12/12/2011 09:39:01]
Re: Conclusion Of NASA Kecksburg Lawsuit

  it's an investigation to keep active.

  Steve

UFO Case Files Of Scotland - A Review




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Nov > Nov 14

                                             UFO Case Files Of Scotland - A Review
  From: Philip Mantle <philip.nul>
  Date: Fri, 13 Nov 2009 13:36:54 -0000
  Archived: Sat, 14 Nov 2009 09:42:13 -0500
  Subject: UFO Case Files Of Scotland - A Review

  Review
  UFO Case Files Of Scotland
  Amazing Real Life Alien Encounters
  Author: Malcolm Robinson
  ISBN: 978-1-907126-02-4
  Pagination: 365 pages - illustrated.
  Publisher: Healings of Atlantis
  Price: =A314.99
  The small country of Scotland is known for many things: its
  beautiful scenery, whiskey, the Loch Ness Monster, Braveheart,
  but not UFOs. But this new book: UFO Case Files Of Scotland by
  Scotland's and one of the UK's leading UFO and Paranormal
  researchers, Malcolm Robinson, could well alter all that.
  Malcolm Robinson has been involved first hand as a UFO and
  Paranormal researcher since the late 1980's. A true Scot in
  every sense of the word Malcolm has been at the forefront of
  research on UFOs in Scotland and the only thing that truly
  surprises me about this book is that it is his first one. Having
  read it from cover to cover I can only hope it is the first of
  many.
  UFO Case Files Of Scotland is in no way definitive, it is
  instead an honest reflection of Malcolm's own involvement in UFO
  research in Scotland. It might not be definitive but of the
  cases that it covers it is certainly in-depth. I have read
  literally hundreds of UFO books down the years and I can
  honestly say, in the best possible way that I can, that this
  book is like no other. Not only does Malcolm go into great
  detail of the case histories featured, but he also gives a very
  graphic description of the mood and atmosphere of the time.
  Details of lectures and conferences are covered, one or two of
  the more humorous moments also, and Malcolm is also man enough
  in this book to hold his hand up when he made a mistake or two,
  all of which of course is to his credit.
  The case histories covered are no less fascinating. In the first
  chapter Malcolm briefly outlines how and why he became involved
  in this research in the first place. This is followed by the
  best coverage I have ever read of the Bonnybridge 'UFO hot
  spot'. In the early to mid-1990's, the small town of Bonnybridge
  was the centre of UFO activity in Britain and some may argue the
  world. I'll not spoil it here by highlighting many of the UFO
  sightings investigated by Malcolm and his colleagues save to say
  that I thought I knew a good bit about the Bonnybridge sightings
  but soon realised when reading this chapter that I knew
  virtually nothing.
  Other case histories featured are the Robert Taylor UFO landing
  account in Livingston of November 1979. With physical traces
  left on the witness and the location (marks in the ground) this
  incident was officially investigated by the local police. The
  A70 Incident is Scotland's first reported UFO abduction account
  and it is featured here in full. This particular event is now
  under consideration for a movie by a UK production company. The
  Fife Incident took place in September 1996 and involved several


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/nov/m14-002.shtml[12/12/2011 09:39:04]
UFO Case Files Of Scotland - A Review

  witnesses. A controversial case of UFO entities and a whole lot
  more is again dealt with in-depth by the author. Scotland's and
  probably Britain's best UFO photographic case took place at
  Craigluscar Reservoir in Scotland on February 19th 1994. The
  full story of this daylight UFO photographic case is covered
  with the photograph in question featured in full. The book is
  rounded off with Malcolm's own look at what UFOs may be.
  I have purposely not given too much detail of the UFO case
  histories featured in this book because to offer brief details
  of them simply would not do them justice.
  Like I said at the beginning this is no ordinary UFO book. UFO
  Case Files Of Scotland is a highly significant contribution to
  the UFO research literature. It is written in a different style
  to any UFO book I have ever read and all I can say is that if
  you are only going to buy one UFO book this year then you could
  go a long way before you find a better one than this. Put it on
  your shopping list now.
  This book is available to order via your local bookshop or
  online direct from the publisher:
  www.HealingsOfAtlantis.com
  and of course via Amazon.

  Philip Mantle

  last comment? Did they just pull it out of the air?
  I don't understand your use of "circumstantial", but even if it
  were true, it would be more suitable than research by
  proclamation. Here is a list of claims made by Document Deniers
  (DD) that supposedly show the documents are phony, and then the
  real facts
  1.The type face on the CT memo should have been Elite, not Pica.
  I provided 14 pica documents (there were many more) and was paid
  $1000. by Phil Klass for proving him wrong.
  2. TOP SECRET RESTRICTED was not used by government agencies in
  1954.
  The GAO found a number of examples of TSR and said so in their
  Roswell Report.
  3. All TOP SECRET Documents must have TS Control Numbers.
  Archivists say they have many TS documents which don't. I had
  even published a couple.
  4. Hillenkoetter would never have called himself Admiral.
  I found at the Ike Library many instances of the use of Generic
  ranks including, for example, Brigadier General Goodpaster
  referring to himself as General.
  5,. Astronomer/debunker Menzel could not have been part of such
  a group.
  I discovered at the Harvard Archives that he had very high
  security clearances and did lots of class- ified work for the
  NSA, CIA, FBI, and other agencies and was close to several of
  the MJ-12 members such as Van Bush and Detlev Bronk and Lloyd
  Berkner 6. The typewriter for the 1947 Truman-Forrestal memo was
  from 1960 or later.



http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/nov/m15-001.shtml[12/12/2011 09:39:05]
More MJ-12 [was:'Fourth Kind' Movie Review]

  Forensic Document examiner Mr. Black ascertained that it dated
  from 1940.
  7. I showed that several of the dates were actually correct such
  as the date Twining went to New Mexico was July 7,1947, by
  finding his and his pilot's flight logs. Also found Spaatz' log
  proving one of the plethora was a phony.
  8.I discovered that the slant red pencil mark thru TSR on the CT
  memo was meaningful. Not previously known.
  9. We determined that the CT memo Dictation onion skin paper was
  made in Bid lots by Fox paper in the right time frame and that
  the government bought lots of it.
  10. Joe Nickell claimed the date format for the EBD was wrong. I
  found other examples of the same format. 11. At the archives I
  found proof that a number of dates were correct such as when
  Smith met with Truman.I found proof that NSA Exec. Sec James Lay
  was indeed in touch with Robert Cutler while he was in Europe.
  If the CT memo had been signed or used /S/, it would have been a
  phony.
  I had conversations about the documents and office procedures
  and person- nel with George Elsey who worked for Truman all the
  time he was in the White House. I talked with families of almost
  all the MJ-12 members and with Forrest Pogue, Marshall's
  biographer.
  12. I have been to a total of 20 archives, some many times,
  doing document research. I know of no one else who has even
  looked at Menzel's papers at Harvard. Do you?
  13. I listed a lot of trivial pieces of information not known to
  be true before the documents were received. I asked if certain
  data had been provided to others before it was provided to me..
  Answer was NO.
  In short then, Steve. I have collected and published a great
  deal of actual data obtained, not by sitting at my office and
  making proclamations, but by digging deeper in many places...
  What have you and other DD done?
  >All this being said, I agree that there must have been a group
  >that was acting in a manner similar to MJ-12, throughout the
  >late 40s and 50s, but MJ-12 has too many flaws.
  Let us not forget that because most isotopes are not fissionable
  does not mean none are. Because most people aren't 7' tall
  doesn't mean none are. Because most MJ-12 documents are phony
  doesn't mean none are genuine.

ETs And Modern Physics

                                                        ETs And Modern Physics
  From: Greg Boone <Evolbaby.nul>
  Date: Sat, 14 Nov 2009 13:56:10 EST
  Archived: Sun, 15 Nov 2009 12:38:32 -0500
  Subject: ETs And Modern Physics

  This is a link to a special presentation featuring cosmologist
  and physicist, Lawrence Krauss:
  http://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7ImvlS8PLIo
  Of note to Ufologists is how he so casually touches on the issue
  of extraterrestrial life as though it's such a given that is has
  no speciality regarding it's uniqueness.
  It took me for a loop because it wasn't even an issue of
  importance to the scientist.
  Furthermore, his lecture covers the full gambit of modern day
  theoretical physics but also leaves some big questions.
  Note also how he does cover how scientists often resist change,
  banter theory, and more.
  The jury is still out on the final say regarding physics and as
  soon as we solve the I_know_it_all equation of arrogance we'll
  still be in the dark matter.

  Best,
  Greg
  ufomafia.com/blog

  Stanton,
  I would like you to convince Mark Rodeghier, or perhaps Don
  Berliner (your co-author for Crash at Corona), or even Mike
  Swords in the validity of any of the MJ-12. Like Roswell, I
  don't believe you're going to get at this by your form of logic
  and research. And it's especially tainted by the Document you
  found at the Archives which had flawed provenance (as you recall
  according to the archivists, it did not belong in the box where
  it was found).
  All this being said, I did read your books and they fell short
  of proof in my opinion. And quite honestly, I really don't care
  about what "could've been" or "might've been", and I am
  especially leery of anecdotal information and what it seems to
  indicate. In other words, damn near all the commentary and
  information you hold dear. People are just to unreliable in
  interpretation.
  The conversation that I was having was about people taking bad
  information into account. What do you think of the validity of
  the case mentioned in The Fourth Kind? What about the Doctor
  mentioned in the movie... do you believe that she's real? Do you
  believe that our attempt at scientific research should be
  further clouded by those who take all UFO media information as
  true? What about the End of the World in December, 2012? Have
  you been preparing to go out with a bang and started to spend
  your assets while you have time?
  Stanton, this isn't about you. I think that most ufologists
  believe that MJ-12 has no provenance and my statement is based
  on discussions at Fund's Executive Committee meetings and the
  UFO Leadership group pulled together in 2002 (or 2003) where we
  got together for three days of meetings on the direction of
  ufology research and how to get more scientific involvement. As
  you probably recall, this was with Funding from the Sci-Fi
  Channel, which later died off.
  I honestly have no intention of getting involved in a debate,
  and am slowly extricating myself from this genre as a result of
  the non-direction it's taking.

  Steve

  know or suspect something most of us don't know - and have
  tried, from a decade or more ago, to think what that might be.
  The results of that speculation are at
  www.perceptions.couk.com/uef/friend-foe.html and it's unfinished
  work - that is, you could change any of the implications from
  'external' to 'internal' (to Earth / us / our brains or
  consciousnesses) and still get the same results. Which means we
  couldn't necessarly know where any "aliens" are situated - if
  'site' is a meaningful term in this case.

  You don't like the provenance of the CT memo. My dictionary says
  provenance means origin. We know the origin.
  It was folded and between 2 folders in Box 189 of Entry 267 of
  Record Group 341 at the National Archives. Anybody can look at
  it.
  I determined that that box was first handled in September 1984
  shortly after the last surviving member of MJ-12 died. It was
  kept in the classified vault and first served to the public in
  July 1985. The Archives refused to tell me the names of the
  declassifiers who handled the box.
  Surely you understand that Classified documents cannot merely be
  handed out like traffic tickets. Of course it was planted in Box
  189. The box # was also given on one of the postcards received
  months earlier. No outsider had access to the box.
  You say you want proof. I suggested possibilities, none
  attainable, but why not a preponderance of the evidence as in
  civil cases? Who are you to demand proof?

  and claims and have provided factual - not anecdotal - reasons
  for saying a host are phony. Don't they count?
  I'm the one who talks about my gray basket. I located the
  documents that were emulated. I found the comment about TSR from
  the GAO. I proved that James Lay met with Ike and later the same
  day spoke with him on July 14, 1954. I found Menzel's letters to
  JFK and his unpublished autobiography proving he was up to his
  ears in very highly classified work for decades after WW 2. Has
  anybody else even looked at Menzel's papers at Harvard?
  >Stanton, this isn't about you. I think that most ufologists
  >believe that MJ-12 has no provenance and my statement is based
  >on discussions at Fund's Executive Committee meetings and the
  >UFO Leadership group pulled together in 2002 (or 2003) where we
  >got together for three days of meetings on the direction of
  >ufology research and how to get more scientific involvement. As
  >you probably recall, this was with Funding from the Sci-Fi
  >Channel, which later died off.
  I wasn't at those meetings. Many scientists thought the earth
  was flat, that no flying machine could be operated, that space
  travel was "Utter Bilge". So what? Ignorance is bliss.
  >I honestly have no intention of getting involved in a debate,
  >and am slowly extricating myself from this genre as a result of
  >the non-direction it's taking.
  Steve, you make outrageous claims and now you want to take your
  marbles and go home. That is fine with me. But I see no reason
  to put up with false claims.
  How about the Fund sponsoring a debate about MJ-12?

  Stan

http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/nov/m18-003.shtml[12/12/2011 09:39:13]
NASA & Microsoft Allow Earthlings To Become




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Nov > Nov 18

                                      NASA & Microsoft Allow Earthlings To Become
  From: NASA News <hqnews.nul>
  Date: Tue, 17 Nov 2009 11:12:46 -0700
  Archived: Wed, 18 Nov 2009 07:44:49 -0500
  Subject: NASA & Microsoft Allow Earthlings To Become

  Nov. 17, 2009
  Dwayne Brown
  Headquarters, Washington
  202-358-1726
  dwayne.c.brown.nul
  Guy Webster
  Jet Propulsion Laboratory, Pasadena, Calif.
  818-354-6278
  guy.webster.nul
  RELEASE: 09-268
  NASA AND MICROSOFT ALLOW EARTHLINGS TO BECOME MARTIANS
  PASADENA, Calif. -- NASA and Microsoft Corp. of Redmond, Wash.,
  have collaborated to create a Web site where Internet users can
  have fun while advancing their knowledge of Mars.
  Drawing on observations from NASA's Mars missions, the "Be a
  Martian" Web site will enable the public to participate as
  citizen scientists to improve Martian maps, take part in
  research tasks, and assist Mars science teams studying data
  about the Red Planet.
  "We're at a point in history where everyone can be an explorer,"
  said Doug McCuistion, director of the Mars Exploration Program
  at NASA Headquarters in Washington. "With so much data coming
  back from Mars missions that are accessible by all, exploring
  Mars has become a shared human endeavor. People worldwide can
  expand the specialized efforts of a few hundred Mars mission
  team members and make authentic contributions of their own."
  Participants will be able to explore details of the solar
  system's grandest canyon, which resides on Mars. Users can call
  up images in the Valles Marineris canyon before moving on to
  chart the entire Red Planet. The collaboration of thousands of
  participants could assist scientists in producing far better
  maps, smoother zoom-in views, and make for easier interpretation
  of Martian surface changes.
  By counting craters, the public also may help scientists
  determine the relative ages of small regions on Mars. In the
  past, counting Martian craters has posed a challenge because of
  the vast numbers involved. By contributing, Web site users will
  win game points assigned to a robotic animal avatar they select.
  With a common goal of inspiring digital-age workforce
  development and life-long learning in science, technology,
  engineering and mathematics, NASA and Microsoft unveiled the Web
  site at the Microsoft Professional Developers Conference in Los
  Angeles this week. The site also beckons software developers to
  win prizes for creating tools that provide access to and
  analysis of hundreds of thousands of Mars images for online,
  classroom and Mars mission team use.
  "Industry leaders like NASA and Microsoft have a social
  responsibility as well as a vested interest in advancing science


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/nov/m18-004.shtml[12/12/2011 09:39:14]
NASA & Microsoft Allow Earthlings To Become

  and technology education," said Walid Abu-Hadba, corporate vice
  president of the Developer and Platform Evangelism Group at
  Microsoft. "We are excited to be working with NASA to provide
  new opportunities to engage with Mars mission data, and to help
  spark interest and excitement among the next generation of
  scientists and technologists."
  To encourage more public participation, the site also provides a
  virtual town hall forum where users can expand their knowledge
  by proposing Mars questions and voting on which are the most
  interesting to the community. Online talks by Mars experts will
  address some of the submitted questions. Other features include
  interactive tools for viewing Martian regions and movies about
  people who study Mars in diverse ways.
  "Mars exploration inspires people of all ages, and we are
  especially eager to encourage young people to explore Mars for
  themselves," said Charles Elachi, director of NASA's Jet
  Propulsion Laboratory in Pasadena, Calif. "We are delighted to
  be involved in providing the creative opportunity for future
  explorers to contribute to our understanding of Mars."
  "The beauty of this type of experience is that it not only
  teaches people about Mars and the work NASA is doing there, but
  it also engages large groups of people to help solve real
  challenges that computers cannot solve by themselves," said Marc
  Mercuri, director of business innovation in the Developer and
  Platform Evangelism Group at Microsoft.
  The Mars Exploration Program is managed by JPL for NASA's
  Science Mission Directorate in Washington.
  To enroll as a virtual Martian citizen and start exploring,
  visit:


  http://beamartian.jpl.nasa.gov

  -end-

  Gildas,
  I disagree. I have found much of the information in the MJ-12
  documents to be contrary to my research. But the entire batch
  was ruined for me when I found the term "retrovirus" in a
  supposedly 1952 document: the "1st annual review". The term
  wasn't invented until after 1974. I think this one mistake
  tainted all other MJ-12 documents. I've pointed this out several
  times to both Stan and the Woods. But there's no getting around
  this mistake.
  Of course there is an MJ-12 type organization functioning, just
  as we all suspect. But the MJ-12 documents are still frauds,
  and we need to stop relying on the information they provide.
  We'd be much better off paying close attention to the
  information the abducted (past and present) have provided.
  >Who is doing that? At least, we know that a similar briefing for
  >the President was shown to Linda Howe by Richard Doty, special
  >agent of AFOSI, in the office and on orders of his boss at
  >Kirtland AFB.
  This has all the trappings of a huge show, put on for Linda's
  benefit. Doty was doing his disinformation-best to con her.
  >How could she have invented that meeting and the contents of the
  >document, revealing, among other things, MJ-12 for the first
  >time?
  The meeting happened. She was fooled into believing it was all
  real, but it was all an elaborate staging by Doty and company.
  >Would Steven Kaeser have an explanation for that?
  Of course he would. This is something he and I agree on.
  Deception, money, fame have kept the MJ-12 documents on the
  front page of ufology, but they are no more valid than Robbert
  van den Broeke's predictions.
  >And BTW, the other briefing sent to Shandera was mailed in
  >Albuquerque, I think. Curious coincidence.
  No Gildas, that's the point. They're trying to fool you, and
  make things seem to be important, that aren't.

Meteorite Explosion And Strange Clouds




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Nov > Nov 19

                                            Meteorite Explosion And Strange Clouds
  From: Joachim Koch <lists.nul>
  Date: Thu, 19 Nov 2009 10:00:14 +0100
  Archived: Thu, 19 Nov 2009 05:45:21 -0500
  Subject: Meteorite Explosion And Strange Clouds

  Hello List!
  As forecasters predicted, the Leonid meteor shower peaked during
  the late hours of Nov. 17th, favoring sky watchers in Asia with
  an outburst of 100+ meteors per hour.
  Just as the outburst was dying down, an even bigger event took
  place over the western USA. Something hit Earth's atmosphere and
  exploded with an energy equivalent of 0.5 to 1 kiloton of TNT.
  Witnesses in Colorado, Utah, Idaho and elsewhere say the
  fireball "turned night into day" and "shook the ground" when it
  exploded just after midnight Mountain Standard Time.
  Researchers who are analyzing infrasound recordings of the blast
  say the fireball was not a Leonid. It was probably a small
  asteroid, now scattered in fragments across the countryside.
  Efforts are underway to measure the trajectory of the asteroid
  and guide meteorite recovery efforts.
  See: http://www.ksl.com/?nid=148&sid=8714738
  Approximately 6 hours after the fireball, people in Utah and
  Colorado got another surprise. As the sun rose over those
  states, a twisting electric-blue cloud appeared in the dawn sky:
  See: http://spaceweather.com/
  The cloud strongly resembles artificial noctilucent clouds
  formed at high altitudes by rocket and shuttle launches. Yet
  there was no (officially reported) rocket launch at dawn on Nov.
  18th.
  Could the cloud be associated with the fireball? The
  geographical coincidence is certainly striking. Debris from the
  fireball should have dissipated by sunrise, but the cloud
  remains unexplained and a connection to the fireball cannot yet
  be dismissed.

  Found for you by
  Joachim Koch

  Talbott.
  As you say she does genuinely believe in him.
  All she will say is that until she has finished the report about
  Robbert's photos of people who have died, she doesn't think it
  is "productive" for her to get into this discussion.
  She says that when the report is finally posted and the
  skeptics/debunkers pay her the "courtesy" of reading it and
  "thoughtfully considering" what it has to say, then she will do
  her best to answer questions that are raised... particularly if
  people will address their queries to her.
  She seems irked because she claims her critics haven't asked her
  or Robbert directly, before publishing their analysis on the
  web.
  Of course she wouldn't be the first researcher to have so much
  invested in a project that they find it hard to withdraw despite
  evidence of hoaxing.
  But I think it would be fair to wait until she completes her
  full report of Robbert's case before assuming her enthusiasm
  overwhelms her scientific sense.

                                  Abduction Using Implants & Light: Proof of Concept?
  From: Rick Nielsen <nilthchi.nul>
  Date: Thu, 19 Nov 2009 19:56:16 -0800 (PST)
  Archived: Fri, 20 Nov 2009 07:47:19 -0500
  Subject: Abduction Using Implants & Light: Proof of Concept?

  This article from ScienceDaily reminded me of alien abduction
  techniques, including using implants, paralysis, and shades of
  light.
  Specifically, can humans be paralyzed with implants and
  artificial light, (including blue-white light) then re-awakened
  with daylight?
  Here's the reference from:
  http://tinyurl.com/yz64726
  New On-Off 'switch' Triggers And Reverses Paralysis In Animals
  With A Beam Of Light
  -----
  New on-off 'switch' triggers and reverses paralysis in animals
  with a beam of light
  In an advance with overtones of Star Trek phasers and other
  sci-fi ray guns, scientists are reporting development of an
  internal on-off "switch" that paralyzes animals when exposed
  to a beam of ultraviolet light. The animals stay paralyzed
  even when the light is turned off. When exposed to ordinary
  light, the animals become unparalyzed and wake up.
  -----

                                   P-47: Definitive Study 1954 BOAC Labrador Report
  From: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  Date: Sat, 21 Nov 2009 10:21:32 -0500
  Archived: Sat, 21 Nov 2009 10:21:32 -0500
  Subject: P-47: Definitive Study 1954 BOAC Labrador Report



  From: Herb Taylor <herbufo.nul>
  To: PROJECT-1947.nul
  Date: Fri, 20 Nov 2009 16:52:11 -0500
  Subject: Definitive Study Of 1954 BOAC Labrador Report

  To all:
  I'm very happy to bring to the attention of list members the
  publication of what can only be described as THE definitive
  study of the classic 1954 BOAC Labrador report. The title of
  this very important work is The BOAC Labrador Sighting Of June
  29, 1954 and was authored by British researcher Martin Shough.
  This work can be found by logging onto the following site:
  http://www.caelestia.be/BOAC.html
  All serious researchers should avail themselves of this study.
  The study itself is composed of 5 chapters - Chap. 4 is divided into
  three parts, each one discussing a different possible explanation - a
  section re. additional sources, and three appendices.
  We need to take a closer look at Appendix C, entitled Similar
  Reports.
  The two opening sentences describing what this section is all
  about, are as follows:
  "This appendix, compiled by Wim Van Utrecht, represents a first
  attempt toward toward an annotated and illustrated catalogue of
  what we could provisionally designate as 'dark blob cases'.
  In its present state, the catalogue contains the details of six
  eye-witness accounts describing phenomena that bear a striking
  resemblance, both in aspect and behavior, to the UAPs described
  in the BOAC Labrador incident."
  My view is that a whole new area of study has been opened not
  heretofore realized by serious UFO research.

  Herb Taylor

  >Ed
  >How about being specific, Ed. What similar content? What
  >misinformation?
  Stan,
  On page 11 of the SOM-1 we find an Extraterrestrial Technology
  Classification Table and it gives a location to send various
  EBE craft, devices, fragments and weapons. That location
  in all cases is "Area 51 S-4". The date of the SOM1 is April 7,
  1954.
  The problem is that Area 51 was only an abandoned US Air Corps
  runway at that time and wasn't established as a research center
  until 1955. Doesn't this seem like disinformation to you and
  don't you think it calls into question the authenticity of the
  entire SOM1?
  From:
  http://science.howstuffworks.com/area-5113.htm
  -----
  A Brief History of Area 51
  During World War II, the Army Air Corps (precursor to our modern
  Air Force) built several runways in Nevada, including a pair of
  small runways at Groom Lake. They named the spot the Army Air
  Corps Gunnery School. After the 1940s, the runways were
  abandoned.
  In the early 1950s, the CIA entered a partnership with Lockheed
  to develop high altitude aircraft to use helmed the project. He
  formed a department of engineers and test pilots that eventually
  took on the name Skunk Works. The Skunk Works department was
  famous for being very secretive and nearly fanatical in the
  pursuit of their goals.
  The CIA and Johnson both knew that secrecy was critical to their
  success, and so Johnson needed to find a location to develop and


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/nov/m21-008.shtml[12/12/2011 09:39:25]
Re: More MJ-12

  test secret aircraft. He wanted a location that was remote
  enough to avoid notice, yet still close enough to a major city
  so that supplying the facility would not be a monumental task.
  The site would need to be easily accessible by aircraft and out
  of the way of commercial and military flight paths. It would
  also need space to house a sizeable force of military and
  civilian employees.
  In 1955, he traveled to Nevada with test pilot Tony LeVier and
  CIA representative Osmond Ritland to find a good place to use as
  a base of operations for test flights. Ritland trained at the
  Gunnery School and told Johnson about it. Johnson decided the
  location was ideal for their operations.
  Four months later, crews completed the initial construction. U-2
  test flights began and President Eisenhower signed an Executive
  Order restricting the airspace over Groom Lake. The CIA, the
  Atomic Energy Commission and Lockheed oversaw base operations.
  Eventually, control of the base would pass to the Department of
  Energy and the Air Force
  -----

  conclusions in this report? Surely the knowledge (or most of it)
  was available in the mid to late 1950s?

  >>>childish and under-drawn and would be totally useless in most
  >>>cases. They consist of images that anyone who has studied UFO
  >>>could have drawn. Certainly no new information there. But why
  >>>the need for drawing and diagrams when they had the real
  >>>wreckage to go by? Why not take a photo?
  >>Ed
  >>How about being specific, Ed. What similar content? What
  >>misinformation?
  >Stan,
  >On page 11 of the SOM-1 we find an Extraterrestrial Technology
  >Classification Table and it gives a location to send various
  >EBE craft, devices, fragments and weapons. That location
  >in all cases is "Area 51 S-4". The date of the SOM1 is April 7,
  >1954.
  >The problem is that Area 51 was only an abandoned US Air Corps
  >runway at that time and wasn't established as a research center
  >until 1955. Doesn't this seem like disinformation to you and
  >don't you think it calls into question the authenticity of the
  >entire SOM1?
  >From:
  >http://science.howstuffworks.com/area-5113.htm
  >-----
  >A Brief History of Area 51
  >During World War II, the Army Air Corps (precursor to our modern
  >Air Force) built several runways in Nevada, including a pair of
  >small runways at Groom Lake. They named the spot the Army Air
  >Corps Gunnery School. After the 1940s, the runways were
  >abandoned.
  >In the early 1950s, the CIA entered a partnership with Lockheed
  >to develop high altitude aircraft to use helmed the project. He
  >formed a department of engineers and test pilots that eventually
  >took on the name Skunk Works. The Skunk Works department was


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/nov/m22-001.shtml[12/12/2011 09:39:27]
Re: More MJ-12

  >famous for being very secretive and nearly fanatical in the
  >pursuit of their goals.
  >The CIA and Johnson both knew that secrecy was critical to their
  >success, and so Johnson needed to find a location to develop and
  >test secret aircraft. He wanted a location that was remote
  >enough to avoid notice, yet still close enough to a major city
  >so that supplying the facility would not be a monumental task.
  >The site would need to be easily accessible by aircraft and out
  >of the way of commercial and military flight paths. It would
  >also need space to house a sizeable force of military and
  >civilian employees.
  >In 1955, he traveled to Nevada with test pilot Tony LeVier and
  >CIA representative Osmond Ritland to find a good place to use as
  >a base of operations for test flights. Ritland trained at the
  >Gunnery School and told Johnson about it. Johnson decided the
  >location was ideal for their operations.
  >Four months later, crews completed the initial construction. U-2
  >test flights began and President Eisenhower signed an Executive
  >Order restricting the airspace over Groom Lake. The CIA, the
  >Atomic Energy Commission and Lockheed oversaw base operations.
  >Eventually, control of the base would pass to the Department of
  >Energy and the Air Force
  >-----
  Dr. Robert Wood found evidence of a massive building project in
  that general area starting in about 1951. Sounds way too fast
  for a sizable underground facility.
  Stan Friedman

  Stan,

  research and then we'll see if that morphs into Area 51S4. Could
  you provide it for the List and me?
  But you requested that I be "specific, Ed", and provide proof of
  "misinformation". I provided the evidence you demanded. The
  mention of Area 51 in the SOM-1 indicates that the entire
  document is as phony as the 1st Annual Report and the other
  mass of MJ-12 misinformation. A much better method of
  approaching this material would be trying to figure out what
  they, the misinformers, are trying to lead us to think.
  Also you and the List might want to consider the following:
  "The earliest reference I have yet seen that uses the term
  "Area 51" is an issue of the N.T.S. Bulletin (Vol.IV No.2) dated
  15 January 1960. On page 1, there is a list of "changes in Area 51
  telephone numbers."
  I suspect now that Area 51 was designated as such when the land
  was withdrawn from public use in 1958. The parcel of land that
  was withdrawn, is the exact same parcel that is designated Area
  51 on Nevada Test Site maps from the 1960s and 1970s.
  The most recent usage of the term seems to date to about 1978."
  (Peter Merlin, Area 51 historian)

  Ed

  It's official, then. I have no sense of humor.
  How funny is a reinforcement that UFOs are only seen by
  credulous and uneducated rednecks living in squalid locations
  likely infested with 'recovering' alcoholics and meth
  manufacturers, who, by the way, will _reflexively_ take a hub-
  cap for a Trufo. A laugh riot, really. Good work, Larry The
  Cable Guy.
  Albeit, my experience is 45 years out of date, but I suspect
  that UFOs are about as 'funny' as 'five dried on an empty
  stomach in silent darkness'. The boundary dissolution involved
  with real contact, I've come to understand from demonstrably
  credible experts and equally expert authorities, precludes the
  ready chuckle, eh? Additionally, ignorance has _ever_ been a
  poor source for quality humor, even through the laughter of glad
  derision.
  Still, regard the appallingly un-regardable we must, is my
  intuition, for our species to survive, at all. Consequently,
  it's my feeling that humor with regard to UFOs should only be
  used to get you through the fear, not give you an excuse to
  pointedly ignore that precipitant of that fear, I'm betting.
  I say all this marveling along with Mr. Boone how cleverly the
  piece was produced - that Pixar stuff always drops me to my
  knees - but this other cut needs to be made just to remind us we
  lack humility about ourselves and are cowardly in the aggregate
  with regard to that lack of humility.

SDI #469 - 20 Questions

                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Nov > Nov 23

                                                         SDI #469 - 20 Questions
  From: Alfred Lehmberg <alienview.nul>
  Date: Sun, 22 Nov 2009 17:03:42 -0600
  Archived: Mon, 23 Nov 2009 10:27:02 -0500
  Subject: SDI #469 - 20 Questions

  SDI #469

  20 Questions inspired at long, last:
  1. What is the real legacy of Richard Hall?
  2. What was, and is, Richard Hall's reward?
  3. What is owed Richard Hall?
  4. Where is Richard Hall Found?
  5. Did Richard Hall know science and internalize its
     philosophy?
  6. Did Richard Hall put his faith in the system even as it
     abused him?
  7. Is the contribution of Richard Hall as yet fully realized?
  8. A question not asked, though begged, is who needs to grovel
     at the feet of the memory of Richard Hall in the first place
     and wash their feet before standing on the shoulders of same,
     in the second?
  9. Why can Richard Hall, like James McDonald, be forgiven, even
     congratulated, for the only flaw either of them had in equal
     measure?
  10. Are UFO organizations set against one another, past,
      present, and future, by the admitted non-admitted
      unilateral, even fascist, government?
  11. Are UFOs ignored because they are too "hard" to
      countenance? How hard is it, and what groups find it
      hardest?
  12. What did Richard Hall find objectionable in Ufology?
  13. Has David Cherniack produced a 3 DVD box set of his, UFOs:
      The Secret History, and does it contain a collection of
      credible Ufologists seldom heard from but as significant as
      any?
  14. What is the impact of UFOs: The Secret History?
  15. Is the economy good or bad for UFOs?
  16. How close to "the truth" is UFOs: The Secret History?
  17. Has UFOs: The Secret History, gotten any untoward
      attention from irksome klasskurtxians? If not why not?
  18. Is the late abductioneer John Mack heavily featured in the
      bonus discs for UFOs: The Secret History, among significant
      others?
  19. Is the extraterrestrial hypothesis examined in a scholarly


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/nov/m23-007.shtml[12/12/2011 09:39:33]
SDI #469 - 20 Questions

        manner within the bonus discs?
  20. Is, apart from the documentary proper, rabid speculation
      rationally regarded in the bonus discs?
  Zounds and Mirgatroid listener, but over an hour of graspable
  outr=E9 remains? Cherniack continues and Lonnie Zamora and Mac
  Tonnies are remembered. In one of the several clips provided...
  Errol's Bobbi makes her report regarding oddnesses from back in
  the day vicinity "Go Home Lake," in Canada... Grant Cameron
  reveals one Turner Holt, President Roosevelt and his Secretary
  of State Cordell Hall -and Holt's cousin- involving proclaimed
  aliens in big glass jugs and a crashed saucer in the Capital
  Building of the time, 1935. "Somebody should know about this
  stuff," Holt's lucid, moral, and now elderly daughters report...
  plus Lehmberg walks you to your carriage outside ranting the
  usual jeremiad of buxom seditions and unsubtle heresies...
  Ufological sensibilities empowered are the listener's own!
  Listen!

                                   Ted Phillips At Niagara County Community College
  From: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  Date: Fri, 27 Nov 2009 12:04:29 -0500
  Archived: Fri, 27 Nov 2009 12:04:29 -0500
  Subject: Ted Phillips At Niagara County Community College



  Source: The Buffalo News
  http://www.buffalonews.com/258/story/875164.html
  November 27, 2009

  UFO Researcher Coming To Niagara County Community College
  A well known UFO researcher, considered one of the "Founding
  Fathers" of UFO studies, will speak next week at Niagara County
  Community College.
  Ted Phillips, director of the Center for Physical Trace
  Research, will speak at 7 p.m. Wednesday in room E-142 of the
  college.
  Phillips, a field engineer and professional photographer, has
  collected and analyzed more than 3,000 UFO landing and material-
  evidence cases.
  He has been directly involved in many famous cases. His
  investigation of a reported landing at Delphos, Kan., is
  considered a model for scientific investigation of UFO landings
  and trace-evidence analysis. At Delphos, a glowing soil ring
  left after the reported UFO touched down yielded evidence of
  massive chemical changes in soil and plant life, and adverse
  health effects on humans and animals that came in contact with
  it.
  Phillips has appeared on television numerous times, notably in
  the series "In Search Of...," in the current SciFi Channel "UFO
  Hunters" series, as well as on several History Channel
  documentaries.
  He is presently investigating a Midwestern site called Marley
  Woods, where UFO sightings, strange light orbs, mystery animal
  sightings and domestic animal-mutilation cases have all been
  reported.
  Phillips will be preceded by a short introduction on UFO studies
  by Dr. Philip Haseley of NCCC's department of anthropology. A
  voluntary donation of $3 for Niagara Hospice will be collected
  at the door. Phillips' appearance is sponsored by NCCC Student
  Government.

  [Thanks to Greg Boone for the lead]

  From: Kathy Kasten <catraja.nul>
  Date: Fri, 27 Nov 2009 21:00:58 +0000
  Archived: Sat, 28 Nov 2009 08:29:36 -0500
  Subject: Extraordinary Knowing

  A proposal and thought for the coming year. Based on the fact
  that simply collecting case reports doesn't provide the solution
  to the UFO problem. I suggest another approach. One that has
  been suggested by others who have come before, but not pursued.
  The Forward to Extraordinary Knowing, by Dr. Elizabeth Mayer, is
  written by Freeman Dyson. Substitute the word ESP with the word
  extraordinary flying phenomena or extraordinary beings.

  [Begin Citation]
  "If one believes, as I do, that ESP exists but is scientifically
  untestable, one must believe that the scope of science is
  limited. I put forward, as a working hypothesis, that ESP is
  real but belongs to a mental universe that is too fluid and
  evanescent to fit within the rigid protocols of controlled
  scientific testing. I do not claim that this hypothesis is true.
  I claim only that it is consistent with the evidence and worthy
  of consideration."
  [End Citation]

  I suggest that the phenomena involves a very different agreed
  upon reality from ours. However, at the fringe of our reality
  there is the possibility of interacting with a separate but
  equal reality. How it can be proved that such an interaction is
  possible? I guess the proof is the difficult part and no UFO
  researcher wants to touch it. It is much easier to report cases
  and not try to solve the problem. So, 60 or a hundred years
  later, there are still the reports but no solution.
  The bottomline is that the only reporting channel is the human
  one - bringing with it personality baggage, including emotional
  baggage, sub rosa agendas, religious bias, etc. Oh sure, it is
  claimed there is unexplained radar blips. However, that data,
  after years, doesn't produce results.
  Research requires a serious study of the humans reporting, their
  emotional state, how they view what they are sensing/seeing. It
  means UFO researchers need to study human physiology,
  psychology, sociology, and all the other 'ologys' relating to how
  humans respond to unusual phenomena. Yeah, sure, it is much
  easier to collect reports but that has led nowhere.

  KK

  At:

  From: Jerome Clark <jkclark.nul>
  Date: Sat, 28 Nov 2009 08:46:18 -0500

  >upon reality from ours. However, at the fringe of our reality

  >and not try to solve the problem. So, 60 or a hundred years
  >later, there are still the reports but no solution.
  It is simply not true that "no UFO researcher wants to touch
  it." In fact, whole books (e.g., Kelleher and Knapp's The Hunt
  for the Skinwalker) have addressed the subject, sometimes quite
  strikingly. Jacques Vallee's books discuss the matter, and so do
  many others. I have written about experientially real but
  otherwise unprovable anomalous encounters and suggested how we
  may go about documenting them. People in this field keep
  thinking they've invented the wheel, unaware that the wheel was
  invented long ago. Ufology's problem is not so much lack of
  ideas (there are zillions of ideas out there, good, awful, and
  middling) as lack of resources.
  >The bottomline is that the only reporting channel is the human
  >one - bringing with it personality baggage, including emotional
  >baggage, sub rosa agendas, religious bias, etc. Oh sure, it is
  >claimed there is unexplained radar blips. However, that data,
  >after years, doesn't produce results.


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/nov/m28-004.shtml[12/12/2011 09:39:43]
Re: Extraordinary Knowing


  I empathically disagree that the data don't produce results. We
  do know a fair amount about how UFOs behave, how they appear,
  what they do and do not do, and so on. Radar data demonstrate a
  fundamental point which it is indefensible to overlook: that the
  core phenomenon (the hard-evidence one) is physical, evidently
  technological, and susceptible to instrumental verification.
  That gives us a huge starting point in our effort to understand
  at least one class of UFO reports.
  >Research requires a serious study of the humans reporting, their
  >emotional state, how they view what they are sensing/seeing. It
  >means UFO researchers need to study human physiology,
  >psychology, sociology, and all the other 'ologys' relating to how
  >humans respond to unusual phenomena. Yeah, sure, it is much
  >easier to collect reports but that has led nowhere.
  What evidence do you have that personalities and UFO experiences
  are related? I haven't seen any, beyond the trivial (such as
  that the mystically inclined are more likely to have contactee
  [as opposed to abductee] experiences than those who are not, and
  even then there are notable exceptions). I'm not saying you're
  entirely wrong, only that if a productive discussion is to
  ensue, you need to think things through and offer up some
  evidence.
  I agree that there are aspects to UFO phenomena that are
  extraordinarily strange and deserving of attention. But to focus
  without good reason and empirical justification on witnesses as
  opposed to phenomena seems to me a dubious allocation of already
  scarce resources, likely to lead nowhere. At its most extreme
  (see literature, debunking) it amounts to a blame-the-victim
  strategy.

  Jerry Clark

Alien Poked His Tongue Out!




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Nov > Nov 28

                                                     Alien Poked His Tongue Out!
  From: Dave Haith <visions1.nul>
  Date: Sat, 28 Nov 2009 20:46:31 -0000
  Archived: Sat, 28 Nov 2009 16:33:24 -0500
  Subject: Alien Poked His Tongue Out!

  Two weird cases from the UK discussed here in this YouTube
  segment from Linda Moulton Howe speaking on Coast to Coast.
  http://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-eg0feLf48g
  She talks of a guy who claims to have videod a water buffalo
  abduction up into a craft (pretty unconvincing video!) and the
  other bizarre case of an alien who twice stuck out his tongue at
  a witness!
  How more strange will these cases get?
  I'm not sure whether I believe the guy who was tongued at -
  maybe he's one of those who wants five minutes of UFO fame.
  And judging by the distance from which he says he observed the
  craft it seems too far to see this alien in detail through a
  window.
  If it happened it is surely an interdimensional thing.
  On the other hand, it's difficult to believe any hoaxer would
  expect to encourage belief by coming up with a story about an
  alien poking his tongue out and smiling.
  Why didn't Linda ask about that smile? I've never heard of a
  grey smiling before - didn't know they had the muscles to do it.
  Wonder if he showed any teeth?
  I asked a pal about the tongue poking thing - I didn't expect a
  mini-thesis on the subject...
  Here's what he wrote after some internet research...
  "These are possible reasons for sticking your tongue out at
  someone.
  "Bleaaghh" That tastes AWFUL. Something taste so vile that you
  would even put a distance between the rest of your body and your
  extremity which tasted it, in this case your tongue.
  There is a difference between that natural response to bad
  tasting food and when children stick out their tongue.
  A juvenile sticks out his or her tongue as an act of aggression,
  especially if accompanied by a blow of air through the pursed
  lips and the extended tongue.
  This, for me, is a representative act of spitting at the other
  person or child - a definite act of aggression. It has been
  banned, frowned upon, discouraged by the adults , and at worst,
  is a act punishable by parents.
  Through centuries, we have held back our instincts to use
  spitting as a weapon. So overt spitting has evolved into the
  civilized sticking out of tongue and hissing.
  This remembered trait therefore is only seen in childhood. Much
  of the discouragement will be drummed into the youngster and it


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/nov/m28-005.shtml[12/12/2011 09:39:43]
Alien Poked His Tongue Out!

  will be gone by the time he or she is starting adolescence.
  So the reasons why we stick out our tongues
  1. Disgust. " vile, horrible, YUCKY"
  2. Aggression. I spit on you, and a 3rd more positive one
  according to the other researchers.
  To the Maoris it is "I'll show you my open mouth so welcome you
  to my village. See, I am not hiding any poison barb underneath
  my tongue to blow into your neck when your back is turned!"
  But here is my imagined reason for sticking the tongue out
  It's a sign of your state of health... so it could be either and
  invitation or a threat depending on whether the tongue was
  healthy or sickly.
  Also, when early man was living in small groups or pack, the
  males, when showing aggression, mistrust or fear would stand
  erect, thus exposing the genitals. In modern society, this would
  be highly frowned on so showing the tongue takes the place of
  showing the genitals, though it has exactly the same meaning.
  It means you are a bad taste and 'Look I am so contemptuous of
  you I will expose my most precious secret tongue (that looks
  like my genitals) out at you and challenge you to bite it-grasp
  it' but 'I will withdraw it quickly so that you cannot!'
  It's all very fascinating but hardly an adult behaviour for a
  sophisticated alien!
  It probably says a lot about the alien and perhaps even more
  about the recipient?"

  Dave Haith

  From: Rick Nielsen <nilthchi.nul>
  Date: Sat, 28 Nov 2009 14:05:44 -0800 (PST)
  Archived: Sun, 29 Nov 2009 06:44:12 -0500
  Subject: Re: Extraordinary Knowing

  >From: Kathy Kasten <catraja.nul>
  >To: post to UFOupdates <post.nul>
  >Date: Fri, 27 Nov 2009 21:00:58 +0000
  >Subject: Extraordinary Knowing
  >A proposal and thought for the coming year.
  <snip>
  >I suggest that the phenomena involves a very different agreed
  >upon reality from ours. However, at the fringe of our reality
  >there is the possibility of interacting with a separate but
  >equal reality. How it can be proved that such an interaction is
  >possible? I guess the proof is the difficult part and no UFO
  >researcher wants to touch it. It is much easier to report cases
  >and not try to solve the problem. So, 60 or a hundred years
  >later, there are still the reports but no solution.
  >The bottomline is that the only reporting channel is the human

  >baggage, sub rosa agendas, religious bias, etc. Oh sure, it is
  >claimed there is unexplained radar blips. However, that data,

  >Research requires a serious study of the humans reporting, their
  >emotional state, how they view what they are sensing/seeing. It
  >means UFO researchers need to study human physiology,
  >psychology, sociology, and all the other 'ologys' relating to how
  >humans respond to unusual phenomena. Yeah, sure, it is much
  >easier to collect reports but that has led nowhere.
  >KK
  Can the imagined be physically manifested? Yes. We do it all the
  time with group efforts, machines, and processes.
  Can the imagined be physically manifested by will alone? Maybe.
  Some things, like increasing or decreasing one's own autonomic
  responses, can be done by focused will alone. No one knows if
  this can be done outside one's own skin, with regularly
  repeatable results. Many have tried. But there appear to to be
  no records of repeatable results. And we humans thrive on
  records of repeatable results.
  Possibly, record-keeping is the thing that defines us as human,
  compared to other animal life.
  And the implied goal of all that record-keeping appears to be to
  understand _and anticipate_ those repeatable patterns that
  surround us, while including all that human baggage.

  I've often wondered the same thing. However, whenever I get a
  report that "doesn't fit", I have to consider it as a probable
  hoax until proven otherwise.
  For example, reports of chubby alien grays that are smiling and
  alien grays that are smiling with a cross on their foreheads
  just don't make sense. Why would people submit reports like
  those?
  Alien sticking his tongue out and smiling? That's a new one.

  Kelly

                                                  Re: Alien Poked His Tongue Out!
  From: Jason Gammon <boyinthemachine.nul>
  Date: Sun, 29 Nov 2009 15:37:59 -0500
  Archived: Mon, 30 Nov 2009 10:47:01 -0500
  Subject: Re: Alien Poked His Tongue Out!

  >From: Kelly Freeman <khfsufo.nul>
  >To: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  >Date: Sun, 29 Nov 2009 10:19:48 -0500
  >Subject: Re: Alien Poked His Tongue Out!
  >>From: Dave Haith <visions1.nul>
  >>To: UFO UpDates <post.nul>
  >>Date: Sat, 28 Nov 2009 20:46:31 -0000
  >>Subject: Alien Poked His Tongue Out!
  >>Two weird cases from the UK discussed here in this YouTube
  >>segment from Linda Moulton Howe speaking on Coast to Coast.
  >>http://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-eg0feLf48g
  <snip>
  >>On the other hand, it's difficult to believe any hoaxer would
  >>expect to encourage belief by coming up with a story about an
  >>alien poking his tongue out and smiling.
  >Hello Dave and list,
  >I've often wondered the same thing. However, whenever I get a
  >report that "doesn't fit", I have to consider it as a probable
  >hoax until proven otherwise.
  >For example, reports of chubby alien grays that are smiling and
  >alien grays that are smiling with a cross on their foreheads
  >just don't make sense. Why would people submit reports like
  >those?
  >Alien sticking his tongue out and smiling? That's a new one.

  Hi Kelly,
  I wouldn't immediately label such cases as hoaxes too quickly.
  Of course this doesn't mean that we should automatically believe
  them at face-value.
  From what I've learned, consciously recalled abduction memories
  are full of confabulation and fantasy mistaken for reality.
  I recently listened to an interview with David Jacobs where he
  discussed a case of a woman who claimed to have seen Abraham
  Lincoln aboard a craft. Under Hypnosis such claim fell away and
  the woman was disappointed to realize what she mistook for Abe
  Lincoln was in fact an alien being.
  So the key thing to take from this is that we need to be
  skeptical and not automatically believe or accept at face value.
  As far as I'm concerned, I can't stand the folks in the UFO
  crowd who automatically believe every singe claim anyone makes
  regarding the phenomena.

  -Jason

                                                      Re: Extraordinary Knowing
  From: William Treurniet <wtreurniet.nul>
  Date: Sun, 29 Nov 2009 22:11:51 -0500
  Archived: Mon, 30 Nov 2009 10:48:36 -0500
  Subject: Re: Extraordinary Knowing

  >From: Stanton T Friedman <fsphys.nul>
  >To: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  >Date: Sun, 29 Nov 2009 12:24:55 -0400
  >Subject: Re: Extraordinary Knowing
  >>From: Kathy Kasten <catraja.nul>
  >>To: post to UFOupdates <post.nul>
  >>Date: Fri, 27 Nov 2009 21:00:58 +0000
  >>Subject: Extraordinary Knowing
  >>A proposal and thought for the coming year. Based on the fact
  >>that simply collecting case reports doesn't provide the solution
  >>to the UFO problem. I suggest another approach. One that has
  >>been suggested by others who have come before, but not pursued.
  >>The Forward to Extraordinary Knowing, by Dr. Elizabeth Mayer, is
  >>written by Freeman Dyson. Substitute the word ESP with the word
  >>extraordinary flying phenomena or extraordinary beings.
  >>[Begin Citation]
  >>"If one believes, as I do, that ESP exists but is scientifically
  >>untestable, one must believe that the scope of science is
  >>limited. I put forward, as a working hypothesis, that ESP is
  >>real but belongs to a mental universe that is too fluid and
  >>evanescent to fit within the rigid protocols of controlled
  >>scientific testing. I do not claim that this hypothesis is true.
  >>I claim only that it is consistent with the evidence and worthy
  >>of consideration."
  >>[End Citation]
  >>I suggest that the phenomena involves a very different agreed
  >Kathy, it's "phenomena involve" or "phenomenon involves."
  >"Phenomena" is plural, "phenomenon" singular.
  What are the odds that Stan and Jerome would use identical wording?
  Am I a little slow in wondering if this is Stan's way of poking fun
  at Jerome for being so picky?
  >>upon reality from ours. However, at the fringe of our reality
  >>there is the possibility of interacting with a separate but
  >>equal reality. How it can be proved that such an interaction is
  >>possible? I guess the proof is the difficult part and no UFO
  >>researcher wants to touch it. It is much easier to report cases
  >>and not try to solve the problem. So, 60 or a hundred years
  >>later, there are still the reports but no solution.
  >I think this makes little sense.
  >We have almost 4000 physical Trace cases from almost 90
  >countries. We have a number of Multiple witness radar visual
  >reports. There are a number of large scale scientific studies.
  >There are many heavily redacted Documents, testimony from people
  >who handled strange wreckage.
  >Your suggestion sounds much like that in the infamous Robert Low
  >memo at the University of Colorado... have the psychologists


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/nov/m30-002.shtml[12/12/2011 09:39:47]
Re: Extraordinary Knowing

  >study the observers not the evidence as to what was observed.
  >That is not science for me. I am interested in flying saucers...
  >not nearly so much in the people who see them.
  Consistent with what Kathy was suggesting is the idea that
  observers may be actively involved in the creation of some
  sightings. The resulting object may still have physical
  attributes that can leave physical traces and reflect radar
  signals. So studying the observer as part of a system producing
  the UFO is not out of line. This would be different from
  evaluating the "psychology and sociology of persons and groups
  who report seeing UFO’s" as suggested in the Low memo.
  UFO researchers generally don't know what to do with reports
  like the 2007 Canary Island sighting described at
  http://www.ufodigest.com/news/1007/tenerife.html
  The report says "Now comes a part that is difficult to disclose
  because it is so unacceptable to many. We decided to verbally
  and mentally ask these objects to stop and hover so that we
  could observe them better. Most of the objects ignored this
  request. But 5 of these objects did respond. Four of these
  objects stopped over the airport area, reversed their direction
  and headed directly to our home. They stopped and hovered a few
  blocks from our home. The fifth object directly over our home,
  in fact directly over my wife who was then asking them to do
  exactly that. I could see the object directly over her at about
  500 feet. An oblong-shaped light. Silent. Obliging."
  There are also Steven Greer's claims that his group can make
  luminous orb phenomena appear following some appropriate mental
  exercises.
  Stan's position seems to be that flying saucers are just
  futuristic versions of human technology, and any other
  hypothesis be damned. This is clear justification for dismissing
  such reports. However, although some sightings may well be craft
  from other planets piloted by physical beings, other sightings
  may not have such a simple explanation. And as Jerome pointed
  out in an earlier contribution to this thread, a number of UFO
  researchers over the years have agreed.
  Perhaps Kathy is impatient because a research methodology
  suitable for pursuing this hypothesis has not been developed. Or
  maybe one has been developed (by Greer, for example), but is
  considered too outlandish to take seriously.

  William
